### Summary Statistics

### 1. AGENTS.md vs. linear-workflow.md: Field Count Conflict

**CRITICAL DISCREPANCY FOUND:**

**AGENTS.md:19** states:
```
the skill enforces the four-field check (goal / success criterion / affected repos / appetite)
```

**rules/linear-workflow.md:11–31** defines six required fields:
```
Goal
Key results (with sub-fields: baseline, target, window, source)
Affected repos
Appetite
Kill condition
Project type
```

**The conflict:** AGENTS.md claims initiatives need only 4 fields for the check; linear-workflow.md declares 6 fields are required before an initiative can enter a cycle (line 11: "An initiative is ready to enter a cycle when it can answer all six of these fields"). 

**Reference in skill:** `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` cites `rules/linear-workflow.md` (line: "initiative definition, lifecycle, cycle model, ops slot (source of truth; six-field shape and KR sub-field requirements)") — confirming that the skill itself implements the six-field model, not the four-field model stated in AGENTS.md.

**Drift severity:** HIGH — the operational documentation AGENTS.md contradicts both the canonical rule file AND the actual skill implementation.

---

### 2. CLAUDE.md Import Lines and Relationship

**CLAUDE.md:5** contains the only import:

```

**Relationship:**
- CLAUDE.md is Claude-Code-specific wrapper; AGENTS.md is tool-agnostic
- CLAUDE.md:3 states explicitly: "Tool-agnostic repository instructions live in `AGENTS.md` — this file imports them and adds Claude-Code-specific notes below"
- CLAUDE.md adds:
  - Skill development conventions (frontmatter, location rules)
  - Repository structure documentation  
  - Hook authoring rules
  - Commit style (with addition: "Do not add `Co-Authored-By` trailers" — which AGENTS.md:38 also states)
  - Key constraints (no stack-prescriptive content, no verbatim copy, voice consistency, reference to `rules/eng-principles-universal.md`)

**Import dependency:** CLAUDE.md depends entirely on AGENTS.md being read first; it is not standalone.

### 5. Specific Duplication and Drift Patterns

#### Pattern A: Linear Workflow Summaries (AGENTS.md vs. linear-workflow.md)

**AGENTS.md:17** (Cycles summary):
```
Work is planned in cycles of 3–4 working days. Each cycle pulls 3 goal-oriented 
initiatives plus an ops slot.
```

**rules/linear-workflow.md:71** (Cycle model):
```
A cycle is 3–4 working days of focused work plus 1 planning day.
```

**Duplication:** YES. Content is mostly parallel, not conflicting (both say 3–4 days).

**AGENTS.md:22–24** (On start of issue):
```
- Move to In Progress
- If not in current cycle, assign to it
- Every issue must be either assigned to an initiative project or explicitly in ops slot
```

**rules/linear-workflow.md:122–126** (On start — expanded):
```
Same three points, PLUS adds: "An issue with neither an initiative nor an ops home 
is untracked — don't let this happen" (emphasis added at line 126)
```

**Duplication:** YES, substantial. AGENTS.md is a condensed summary; linear-workflow.md is authoritative.

**AGENTS.md:26–28** (On completion):
```
Move to Done only after work is committed AND pushed. Status updates happen at 
moment of state change. Blocked = leave In Progress + add blocker comment.
```

**rules/linear-workflow.md:128–142** (On completion — expanded):
```
Five-step gate before Done: Review (code-review-and-quality), Fix, Commit+push, 
Summary comment (with review summary), Done. Same status-update-at-moment rule. 
Same Blocked rule.
```

**DRIFT SEVERITY:** MEDIUM — AGENTS.md omits the five-step gate entirely. A new agent reading AGENTS.md would not know about the code-review requirement or the summary-comment step before marking Done.

#### Pattern B: Initiative Field Count (AGENTS.md vs. linear-workflow.md)

**Already detailed in Section 1 above.** This is a CRITICAL drift where AGENTS.md contradicts the canonical rule.

### 7. Critical Issues Summary

| Initiative field count: 4 vs 6 | AGENTS.md:19 vs linear-workflow.md:11 | **CRITICAL** | Agents using AGENTS.md will create incomplete initiatives. Skill actually enforces 6 fields. |

---

### 8. Recommendations for Drift Resolution

1. **Fix AGENTS.md:19 immediately** — change "four-field check" to "six-field check" and list all six fields.
2. **Expand AGENTS.md:26–28** — add the five-step completion gate (Review → Fix → Commit+push → Summary comment → Done).
3. **Consider AGENTS.md as operational summary only** — mark it explicitly as a condensed reference that defers to `rules/linear-workflow.md` for authoritative detail (it already does this at line 15, but the field-count and completion-gate omissions undermine that intent).
4. **Consolidate or clearly separate duplication** — decide whether AGENTS.md duplicates cycle/backlog details for convenience, or whether those sections should be replaced with "see `rules/linear-workflow.md` for full detail."

## GOVERNANCE RULES MAPPING — COMPLETE REPORT

### GLOBAL GOVERNANCE: `/Users/anton/.claude/CLAUDE.md`

**Structure:** The global CLAUDE.md is a hub-and-spoke model. It imports 5 external files (all @-imports are to agent-skills-shaper rules):

1. `@RTK.md` — Token optimization CLI proxy documentation (local)
2. `@/Users/anton/src/agent-skills-shaper/rules/PRODUCT_RULES.md` — Product philosophy & roadmap rules
3. `@/Users/anton/src/agent-skills-shaper/rules/eng-principles-universal.md` — Universal engineering principles (10 principles)
4. `@/Users/anton/src/agent-skills-shaper/rules/eng-principles-agentic.md` — AI-agent-specific principles (7 principles)
5. `@/Users/anton/src/agent-skills-shaper/rules/linear-workflow.md` — Initiative model, cycle composition, issue lifecycle

Then adds **Learning and Memory Management** and **Comments** rules (code comment standards: no ticket IDs, no commit SHAs, no fix-history narration).

**Key insight:** All governance is sourced from agent-skills-shaper's `/rules/` directory except RTK.md (which is local).

---

### REPO-BY-REPO GOVERNANCE STATUS

| Repo | CLAUDE.md | AGENTS.md | /rules/ dir | /.claude/ dir | Governance strategy |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **agent-skills-shaper** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ (4 files) | ✓ | **AUTHORITATIVE SOURCE** for governance rules |
| **agent-skills** | ✓ (shim) | ✓ (standalone) | ✗ | ✓ | Delegates governance via @AGENTS.md; AGENTS.md has own Linear workflow (does NOT reference agent-skills-shaper) |
| **nestl** | ✓ (project-specific) | ✓ (standalone) | ✗ | ✓ | Delegates governance via @AGENTS.md; AGENTS.md has own Linear workflow (does NOT reference agent-skills-shaper) |
| **adyen-onboarding** | ✓ (shim) | ✓ (Tolaria vault conventions) | ✗ | ✓ | Delegates governance via @AGENTS.md; AGENTS.md is a Tolaria note-vault guide (not work governance) |
| **stock-review** | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | No governance rules at all |

---

### THE CRITICAL DIVERGENCE

**agent-skills, nestl, and adyen-onboarding do NOT import agent-skills-shaper rules.** Instead, they each define their own Linear workflow. This creates **three parallel Linear workflows**:

#### **agent-skills/AGENTS.md — Linear workflow** (lines 13–35)
- Personal utility skill pack
- Cycles with 3–4 working days, spans multiple projects
- Explicit cycle management: move to In Progress, assign to cycle if not already in it
- Done = committed AND pushed to main
- Blocked = In Progress + blocker comment
- **No reference to initiative model, no OKR structure**

#### **nestl/AGENTS.md — Linear workflow** (lines 35–53)
- Rails monolith with admin portal
- Cycles with 3–4 working days, spans multiple projects
- Same mechanics: In Progress, current cycle preference
- Done = green tests AND committed AND pushed AND browser-verified (UI tasks)
- **No reference to initiative model, no OKR structure**

#### **agent-skills-shaper/AGENTS.md — Linear workflow** (lines 11–34)
- **References `/rules/linear-workflow.md` as canonical source** — "read it before doing any Linear work"
- Defines initiative model: 6-field check (Goal + Key Results + Affected repos + Appetite + Kill condition + Project type)
- Cycle composition: exactly 3 goal-oriented initiatives + 1 ops slot
- Done = KRs observed, not issues closed
- Governance model = `/rules/linear-workflow.md` (source of truth)
- **Creation gate:** must use `/initiative-shape` skill, enforces six-field check

---

### WHAT'S CENTRALIZED VS. DISTRIBUTED

**Centralized in agent-skills-shaper/rules/:**
- `PRODUCT_RULES.md` — Product principles (outcomes not outputs, problems not solutions, bets not promises, evidence beats opinion, etc.)
- `eng-principles-universal.md` — 10 engineering principles (shipped ≠ done, architecture as reversibility, code as liability, etc.)
- `eng-principles-agentic.md` — 7 agent-specific principles (context as asset, hallucination default, shortest path as failure mode, etc.)
- `linear-workflow.md` — Initiative model with 6-field check, cycle composition rules, issue lifecycle

**Distributed (repo-specific, not imported):**
- `agent-skills/AGENTS.md` — Linear workflow (simplified, no initiative model)
- `nestl/AGENTS.md` — Linear workflow + Rails-specific conventions + UI/DESIGN.md rules
- `adyen-onboarding/AGENTS.md` — Tolaria vault conventions only (not actual work governance)
- Global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` — Learning/memory management + comment standards

**Missing entirely:**
- `stock-review/` — no governance rules of any kind

---

### DUPLICATION & DIVERGENCE SUMMARY

**High divergence:**
- **Linear workflow** is stated THREE different ways (agent-skills, nestl, agent-skills-shaper). The agent-skills and nestl versions do NOT mention initiatives, OKRs, or the 6-field check. They are procedural (cycle management, done criteria) but not strategic.
- **Product rules** exist only in agent-skills-shaper and are NOT imported by the other repos.
- **Engineering principles** (universal & agentic) exist only in agent-skills-shaper and are NOT imported.

**What works:**
- The global CLAUDE.md correctly imports all four rule files from agent-skills-shaper, so any session starting there will load the governance.
- agent-skills-shaper's own AGENTS.md correctly points to `rules/linear-workflow.md` as the source of truth.

**The problem:**
- agent-skills, nestl, and adyen-onboarding have **standalone Linear workflow sections that contradict or simplify** the initiative-based model in agent-skills-shaper/rules/linear-workflow.md
- A human (or agent) working in nestl sees a simpler Linear workflow than someone working in agent-skills-shaper, even though they are the same team/workspace
- No consistency guarantee that these rules are followed across repos

---

1	# AGENTS.md
2	
3	Instructions for any coding agent (Claude Code, Codex, etc.) working in this repository. Tool-agnostic by design — see `CLAUDE.md` for any Claude-Code-specific additions.
4	
5	## What this repo is
6	
7	A personal utility skill pack. Skills live at `<name>/SKILL.md` (flat layout).
8	
9	## Local dev
10	
11	Python version is pinned via `.mise.toml`. With [`mise`](https://mise.jdx.dev) installed, run `mise trust` then `mise install` in the repo root to provision it; `python3` then resolves to the pinned version. The resell-au scripts and their goldens were verified against this version.
12	
13	## Linear workflow
14	
15	Linear is authoritative for status. Local task lists are fine for within-session bookkeeping; they don't replace a Linear issue.
16	
17	**Project:** https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/facebook-listing-skill-pack-f50035279b4c/overview (team ABA / Personal).
18	
19	**Cycles.** Work is planned across cycles, often spanning multiple projects at once. When picking up an issue, prefer ones already in the current cycle. If you start something not in the cycle, decide explicitly whether to pull it in or defer — don't silently expand cycle scope. Use `mcp__linear-server__list_cycles` to see the current cycle.
20	
21	**On start of any issue:**
22	- Move to **In Progress** via `mcp__linear-server__save_issue`.
23	- If the issue isn't yet in the current cycle and you intend to ship it this cycle, assign it to the current cycle.
24	
25	**On completion:**
26	- Move to **Done** only after the work is committed AND pushed to main. An issue isn't Done if the work only exists locally. (This repo pushes directly to main; PRs only when the owner asks.)
27	- Status updates happen at the moment of state change — not batched at end of session.
28	
29	**Blocked** = leave In Progress + add a blocker comment naming the blocker. Don't silently park work.
30	
31	**New work surfaced mid-flight** becomes a new Linear issue, slotted into a cycle deliberately. Don't silently expand scope.
32	
33	## Git
34	
35	Conventional-commit-ish prefixes: `feat:`, `fix:`, `chore:`, `docs:`, `refactor:`. Subject line ≤ 70 chars; details in the body if needed. Do not add `Co-Authored-By` trailers.
36	
```

1	# Nestl — Amsterdam Rental Finder
2	
3	## What this is
4	Personal rental agent that monitors Dutch housing platforms, scores listings against family requirements, and surfaces the best matches.
5	
6	## Tech stack
7	Rails 8 monolith, Hotwire (Turbo + Stimulus), Tailwind CSS, SQLite, Solid Queue. No separate frontend.
8	
9	## Key docs
10	- `docs/prd.md` — high-level product requirements
11	- `docs/adr/` — architecture decision records
12	- `DESIGN.md` — UI design system (authoritative)
13	
14	## Architecture decisions
15	- Rails monolith with Rails 8 defaults (ADR-001)
16	- Platform adapter pattern for multi-site support (ADR-002)
17	- Hotwire over SPA (ADR-003)
18	
19	## Conventions
20	- Rails defaults: Minitest, fixtures, ERB views
21	- Platform adapters live in `app/services/platforms/`
22	- Evaluation pipeline: Ingestion → Bike Time → Work Commute → Deal-Breakers → Scoring
23	- Test stubbing: subclass pattern (no Mocha) — override methods in a Stub* inner class
24	- Services follow enricher/checker patterns: initialize with listing + optional profile, `call` method
25	- UI: follow DESIGN.md strictly (no radius, no shadows, no transitions, IBM Plex only, semantic colors)
26	- Run `bin/rails test` to verify baseline — all green.
27	
28	## UI/frontend changes
29	- Before touching `app/views/**`, `app/helpers/**`, `app/javascript/**`, `app/assets/**`, or Tailwind config: Read DESIGN.md first.
30	- Match the project's existing UI primitives (toolbar filter dropdowns, cards, chips, stat rows) before inventing new layouts. New pattern = justify in spec.
31	- Before declaring any UI task complete: run `bin/dev`, open feature in browser, verify behavior. No exceptions.
32	
33	## Where things live
34	- Services: `app/services/<feature>_<role>.rb` — one class, `call` method
35	- Platform adapters: `app/services/platforms/<name>/{fetcher,parser,search_scraper}.rb`
36	- Jobs: `app/jobs/` — Solid Queue; recurring defined in `config/recurring.yml`
37	- Admin: `app/controllers/admin/` + `app/views/admin/`
38	- Fixtures: `test/fixtures/` (`listings.yml` authoritative); HTML samples in `test/fixtures/files/`
39	
40	## Reference files (read when writing similar)
41	- Service: `app/services/listing_ingester.rb`
42	- Platform adapter: `app/services/platforms/funda/parser.rb`
43	- Job: `app/jobs/funda_search_scrape_job.rb`
44	- Controller: `app/controllers/listings_controller.rb`
45	
46	## Commands
47	- `bin/dev` — dev server (Rails + Tailwind)
48	- `bin/rails test` — full suite
49	- `bin/rubocop` — lint
50	- `bin/rails c` — console
51	- `bin/rails db:migrate` / `db:rollback` — schema
52	
53	## Gotchas
54	- Schema changes via migrations only — never edit `db/schema.rb` directly
55	- SQLite multiplex: `storage/{primary,queue,cache,cable}.sqlite3`
56	- Solid Queue runs in-process in production via `SOLID_QUEUE_IN_PUMA=1`
57	- No Mocha — test stubs via `Stub*` subclass pattern (override methods on a subclass)
58	- Funda fetches are rate-limited; never hit real URLs in tests
59	
```

1	# Nestl — Amsterdam Rental Finder
2	
3	## What this is
4	Personal rental agent that monitors Dutch housing platforms, scores listings against family requirements, and surfaces the best matches.
5	
6	## Tech stack
7	Rails 8 monolith, Hotwire (Turbo + Stimulus), Tailwind CSS, SQLite, Solid Queue. No separate frontend.
8	
9	## Current work
10	Jobs 1-5, 8 complete. Daily search scrape, photo analysis, work commute, listing status tracking all shipped. Admin portal: All jobs (0-5) complete.
11	
12	## Key docs
13	- `docs/prd.md` — high-level product requirements
14	- `docs/technical-spec.md` — data model, FRs, architecture
15	- `docs/jobs-to-be-done.md` — prioritised JTBD + tracer bullet spec
16	- `docs/user-stories-phase1.md` — Phase 1 stories
17	- `docs/adr/` — architecture decision records
18	- `docs/admin-portal-plan.md` + `docs/admin-portal-stories.md` — admin portal spec
19	- `DESIGN.md` — UI design system (authoritative)
20	
21	## Architecture decisions
22	- Rails monolith with Rails 8 defaults (ADR-001)
23	- Platform adapter pattern for multi-site support (ADR-002)
24	- Hotwire over SPA (ADR-003)
25	
26	## Conventions
27	- Rails defaults: Minitest, fixtures, ERB views
28	- Platform adapters live in `app/services/platforms/`
29	- Evaluation pipeline: Ingestion → Bike Time → Work Commute → Deal-Breakers → Scoring
30	- Test stubbing: subclass pattern (no Mocha) — override methods in a Stub* inner class
31	- Services follow enricher/checker patterns: initialize with listing + optional profile, `call` method
32	- UI: follow DESIGN.md strictly (no radius, no shadows, no transitions, IBM Plex only, semantic colors)
33	- Run `bin/rails test` to verify baseline. ~307 tests, 0 known failures.
34	
35	## Linear workflow
36	
37	Linear is authoritative for status. Local task lists are fine for within-session bookkeeping; they don't replace a Linear issue.
38	
39	**Project:** nestl — https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/nestl-3ea6a58e8e19 (team ABA / Personal).
40	
41	**Cycles.** Work is planned across cycles, often spanning multiple projects at once. When picking up an issue, prefer ones already in the current cycle. If you start something not in the cycle, decide explicitly whether to pull it in or defer — don't silently expand cycle scope. Use `mcp__linear-server__list_cycles` to see the current cycle.
42	
43	**On start of any issue:**
44	- Move to **In Progress** via `mcp__linear-server__save_issue`.
45	- If the issue isn't yet in the current cycle and you intend to ship it this cycle, assign it to the current cycle.
46	
47	**On completion:**
48	- Move to **Done** only after `bin/rails test` is green AND the work is committed AND pushed. UI tasks must also be browser-verified per CLAUDE.md. An issue isn't Done if the work only exists on a local branch.
49	- Status updates happen at the moment of state change — not batched at end of session.
50	
51	**Blocked** = leave In Progress + add a blocker comment naming the blocker. Don't silently park work.
52	
53	**New work surfaced mid-flight** (a new platform adapter, refactor, or follow-up bug) becomes a new Linear issue, slotted into a cycle deliberately. Don't silently expand scope.
54	
```

1	---
2	type: Note
3	_organized: true
4	---
5	
6	# AGENTS.md — Tolaria Vault
7	
8	This is a [Tolaria](https://github.com/refactoringhq/tolaria) vault, a folder of Markdown files with YAML frontmatter forming a personal knowledge graph.
9	
10	Keep edits compatible with this starter vault's current conventions. Prefer small, human-readable changes over heavy restructuring.
11	
12	## Core conventions
13	
14	- One Markdown note per file.
15	- The first H1 in the body is the preferred display title. Legacy `title:` frontmatter is still read as a fallback when a note has no H1, but do not add it to new notes.
16	- Store note type in the `type:` frontmatter field.
17	- In this starter vault, type definitions currently live at the vault root, for example `project.md`, `person.md`, `note.md`, and `type.md`. Keep new type files at the vault root unless the user explicitly asks to reorganize them.
18	- Saved views live in `views/*.yml`.
19	- Files in `attachments/` are assets, not notes. Reference them from notes, but do not treat them as notes or types.
20	- Frontmatter properties that start with `_` are usually Tolaria-managed state. Leave them alone unless the user explicitly asks for them to change.
21	
22	## Notes
23	
24	```yaml
25	---
26	type: Note
27	related_to: "[[tolaria]]"
28	status: Active
29	url: https://example.com
30	---
31	
32	# Example note
33	
34	Body content in Markdown.
35	```
36	
37	## Types
38	
39	In this starter vault, types are regular notes stored at the vault root and use `type: Type`.
40	
41	```yaml
42	---
43	type: Type
44	_icon: rocket
45	_color: "#3b82f6"
46	_order: 0
47	_list_properties_display:
48	  - related_to
49	_sort: "property:onboarding:asc"
50	---
51	
52	# Project
53	```
54	
55	Useful type metadata in this vault includes `icon`/`_icon`, `color`/`_color`, `order`/`_order`, `sidebar label`, `_list_properties_display`, `_sort`, `template`, `view`, and `visible`. When editing an existing file, preserve the key style already used there instead of mass-normalizing underscored keys.
56	
57	## Relationships
58	
59	Any frontmatter property whose value contains `[[wikilinks]]` is treated as a relationship. In this starter vault, common relationship keys include `related_to` and `belongs_to`, but custom relationship names are valid too.
60	
61	Preserve older relationship labels such as `Belongs to:` when editing existing notes that already use them.
62	
63	Use quoted wikilinks for scalar frontmatter values and YAML lists for multi-value relationships.
64	
65	## Wikilinks
66	
67	- `[[filename]]` or `[[Note Title]]` for normal links
68	- `[[filename|display text]]` for custom display text
69	- Works in frontmatter values and Markdown body
70	
71	## Views
72	
73	Saved views live in `views/*.yml` and are written as YAML. Tolaria scans every `.yml` file in `views/`, and the filename is the stable view id, so use kebab-case filenames such as `active-projects.yml`.
74	
75	A view definition looks like this:
76	
77	```yaml
78	name: Active Projects
79	icon: null
80	color: null
81	sort: "property:onboarding:asc"
82	filters:
83	  any:
84	    - field: type
85	      op: equals
86	      value: Project
87	    - field: related_to
88	      op: contains
89	      value: "[[tolaria]]"
90	```
91	
92	View rules that matter when creating or editing files:
93	- `name` is required. `icon`, `color`, and `sort` are optional.
94	- `sort` uses `option:direction`. Built-in options are `modified`, `created`, `title`, and `status`. Custom-property sorts use `property:<Property Name>`, for example `property:onboarding:asc`.
95	- `filters` must be a tree whose root is exactly one `all:` group or one `any:` group.
96	- Each filter condition uses `field`, `op`, and usually `value`.
97	- `field` can target built-ins like `type`, `status`, `title`, `favorite`, and `body`, plus actual frontmatter keys used in this vault such as `related_to`, `belongs_to`, or `url`.
98	- Supported operators are `equals`, `not_equals`, `contains`, `not_contains`, `any_of`, `none_of`, `is_empty`, `is_not_empty`, `before`, and `after`.
99	- `any_of` and `none_of` expect `value` to be a YAML list.
100	- `regex: true` is supported with `equals`, `not_equals`, `contains`, and `not_contains` when pattern matching is needed.
101	- Relationship filters can use wikilinks in `value`, for example `"[[tolaria]]"`.
102	- Do not create JSON view files or `.view.json` filenames.
103	
104	## Filenames
105	
106	Use kebab-case: `my-note-title.md`. One note per file.
107	
108	## What agents should do
109	
110	- Create and edit notes using the frontmatter and H1 conventions above.
111	- Create and edit type documents at the vault root.
112	- Add or modify relationships without breaking existing wikilinks.
113	- Create and edit saved views in `views/`.
114	- Update `AGENTS.md` only when the user asks for vault-level guidance changes.
115	
116	## What agents should avoid
117	
118	- Do not move existing type files out of the vault root unless the user explicitly asks.
119	- Do not treat files in `attachments/` as notes, types, or view definitions.
120	- Do not silently overwrite an existing custom `AGENTS.md`.
121	- Do not rewrite installation-specific app configuration unless the user explicitly asks.
122	
```

> AGENT

I now have the full picture, including two issues the summaries didn't capture:

- **nestl** and **agent-skills** restate a *simpler, older* Linear model (no initiative/6-field/ops-slot model) and reference stale MCP tool names (`mcp__linear-server__*`, while this session uses `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). So governance diverges **by tool too**: Claude Code loads the initiative model via the global `@`-imports; Codex reading `AGENTS.md` gets the old simple model.
- **nestl/CLAUDE.md** doesn't `@AGENTS.md` at all, so its own AGENTS.md governance may never load under Claude Code.

The core architecture is clear: `agent-skills-shaper/rules/` must stay the single source (the Shaper pack's own skills cite these files by relative path, and the README lists them as plugin imports — moving them breaks the plugin). The fix is to make *everything else point to it and restate nothing*. Two design decisions genuinely branch the plan, so let me get your call on them.

the canonical model is **six fields**.\n    It also omits the 5-step completion gate.\n  - `agent-skills/AGENTS.md` and `nestl/AGENTS.md` carry a **simpler, older model** (no initiative\n    model, no six-field check, no 3-initiatives+ops-slot cycle) and reference **stale MCP tool names**\n    (`mcp__linear-server__*`; this workspace uses `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`).\n- Divergence is also **by tool**: Claude Code gets the initiative model via the global `@`-imports;\n  Codex (which reads `AGENTS.md`) gets whatever stale model that file restates.\n- `nestl/CLAUDE.md` does **not** `@AGENTS.md`, so its own AGENTS.md governance never loads under Claude Code.\n- Loading all four rule files (~680 lines) into every session is heavy — works against the\n  \"minimal, concise, so the agent doesn't hallucinate or forget\" goal.\n\n**Outcome wanted:** one canonical place for cross-repo governance; everything else *points* to it and\n*restates nothing*; the always-on footprint is small, with deep rules loaded on demand.\n\n**Decisions (confirmed with user):**\n- **Loading: Hybrid** — a short index is always loaded; bulky reference rules load on demand.\n- **Scope: active code repos** — agent-skills-shaper, agent-skills, nestl, + a stub for stock-review.\n  The `adyen-onboarding` Tolaria vault is excluded (not a code project).\n\n**Architecture decision (not changing):** the canonical rules stay in `agent-skills-shaper/rules/`.\nThe Shaper pack *is* the governance product; its own skills cite these files by relative path\n(e.g. `design-doc/SKILL.md:114` \"Load `eng-principles-universal.md` Rules A1, A2…\") and the README\nlists them as plugin imports. Moving them would break the plugin's self-containment.\n\n---\n\n## Design\n\n**Single source of truth:** `agent-skills-shaper/rules/`. Add one thin entry point —\n`rules/GOVERNANCE.md` (~50 lines) — that everything references.\n\nGOVERNANCE.md is **hybrid**:\n- **Inlines** the always-on, short rules: Linear lifecycle mechanics + the comment standard.\n- **Points** (prose, not `@`-import) to the bulky reference rules with a \"read when\" trigger.\n  The Shaper skills already read these on demand.\n\nThe anti-drift mechanism is a single rule, applied everywhere: **define once in `rules/`, reference\neverywhere, restate nowhere.** Each consumer carries a pointer (which can't drift), never a copy.\n\n```\n~/.claude/CLAUDE.md ──@──▶ rules/GOVERNANCE.md ──prose pointers──▶ PRODUCT_RULES.md\nrepo/AGENTS.md ──────────▶ (same file, abs path)                   eng-principles-*.md\n                                                                   linear-workflow.md\n                              inlines: Linear mechanics + comment standard (always-on)\n```\n\n---\n\n## Changes\n\n### 1.   "new_string": "## Verification — run by a subagent, per issue, before each moves to Done\n\nMarkdown-only repos — no build/tests; verification is by inspection. **Each issue's verification is\nperformed by a dedicated verification subagent, not by the implementing agent.** This gives the check\nfresh context and an adversarial second pass (agentic Principle 4: evidence beats vibes; and the\n\"review matters more under agents\" amplification). The implementing agent must **not** self-certify.\n\n**When the Linear issues are written, each one carries its own self-contained verification-subagent\nprompt** containing: (a) the exact checks for that issue (from the list below), (b) the pass criteria,\n(c) an explicit instruction to report **PASS/FAIL with the raw evidence** (actual `grep` output, file\nexcerpts) — never an assertion like \"looks correct\", and (d) a read-only constraint: the subagent\ninspects and reports, it does not edit. On FAIL the issue stays In Progress and goes back to the\nimplementer; the subagent never \"fixes\" to make a check pass.\n\nVerification-subagent model: **Haiku 4.5** for the grep/inspection checks (mechanical); **Sonnet 4.6**\nfor the one semantic check that compares two files for meaning, not strings (Issue 1, check 6).\n\nPer-issue checks the prompts must encode:\n\n1. **(Issue 2) Imports resolve:** `grep -n \"@\" ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` shows one governance import; the\n   four old rule imports and the `## Comments` section are gone; `rules/GOVERNANCE.md` exists.\n2. **(Issues 3–5) No restatements remain:** `grep -rn \"## Linear workflow\"`\n   across `agent-skills-shaper/`, `agent-skills/`, `nestl/` AGENTS.md returns nothing.\n3. **(Issues 1,3) Drift bug gone:** `grep -rn \"four-field\" /Users/anton/src` returns nothing.\n4. **(Issues 4,5) Stale tool names gone:** `grep -rn \"mcp__linear-server__\"` in `agent-skills/`,\n   `nestl/` returns nothing.\n5. **(Issues 3–6) Exactly one pointer per repo:** each touched AGENTS.md has one `rules/GOVERNANCE.md`\n   reference and no inline governance restatement; the reference is the **path-free plugin form**\n   (no `/Users/anton/...` in any checked-out AGENTS.md — `grep -rn \"/Users/anton\" <repo>/AGENTS.md` = 0).\n6. **(Issue 1) Inline ≡ canonical** *(Sonnet)*: GOVERNANCE.md's Linear mechanics match\n   `linear-workflow.md` (six-field, 5-step completion gate, 3-initiatives+ops, Done = KRs observed).\n7. **(Issue 5) nestl link fixed:** `nestl/CLAUDE.md` now `@AGENTS.md`; AGENTS.md carries the merged\n   dev sections with no content lost.\n8. **(Issue 1/global) Hybrid intact:** `skills/design-doc/SKILL.md` \"Load eng-principles-*\" step still\n   points at unchanged `rules/` paths; a fresh session in a non-shaper repo shows GOVERNANCE.md's short\n   index present and the ~680 lines of bulky rules **not** eagerly loaded.\n\nInitiative closes when KR1–KR3 are observed (per the sources above), not when issues hit zero."

{
  "plan": "# Consolidate workspace governance into one source\n\n## Context\n\nGovernance rules are scattered and have already diverged:\n\n- The **global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`** `@`-imports four rule files by absolute path from\n  `agent-skills-shaper/rules/` (PRODUCT_RULES, eng-principles-universal, eng-principles-agentic,\n  linear-workflow), plus inlines a Comment standard and a Memory/journal rule.\n- **Each repo's `AGENTS.md` restates the Linear workflow in its own words** — and the copies\n  contradict the canonical `rules/linear-workflow.md`:\n  - `agent-skills-shaper/AGENTS.md` says **\"four-field check\"**; the canonical model is **six fields**.\n    It also omits the 5-step completion gate.\n  - `agent-skills/AGENTS.md` and `nestl/AGENTS.md` carry a **simpler, older model** (no initiative\n    model, no six-field check, no 3-initiatives+ops-slot cycle) and reference **stale MCP tool names**\n    (`mcp__linear-server__*`; this workspace uses `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`).\n- Divergence is also **by tool**: Claude Code gets the initiative model via the global `@`-imports;\n  Codex (which reads `AGENTS.md`) gets whatever stale model that file restates.\n- `nestl/CLAUDE.md` does **not** `@AGENTS.md`, so its own AGENTS.md governance never loads under Claude Code.\n- Loading all four rule files (~680 lines) into every session is heavy — works against the\n  \"minimal, concise, so the agent doesn't hallucinate or forget\" goal.\n\n**Outcome wanted:** one canonical place for cross-repo governance; everything else *points* to it and\n*restates nothing*; the always-on footprint is small, with deep rules loaded on demand.\n\n**Decisions (confirmed with user):**\n- **Loading: Hybrid** — a short index is always loaded; bulky reference rules load on demand.\n- **Scope: active code repos** — agent-skills-shaper, agent-skills, nestl, + a stub for stock-review.\n  The `adyen-onboarding` Tolaria vault is excluded (not a code project).\n\n**Architecture decision (not changing):** the canonical rules stay in `agent-skills-shaper/rules/`.\nThe Shaper pack *is* the governance product; its own skills cite these files by relative path\n(e.g. `design-doc/SKILL.md:114` \"Load `eng-principles-universal.md` Rules A1, A2…\") and the README\nlists them as plugin imports. Moving them would break the plugin's self-containment.\n\n---\n\n## How this plan is executed\n\n**This plan creates a Linear initiative (project) + issues now; the file changes are executed\nseparately, issue by issue.** On approval, the only actions in *this* session are:\n\n1. Create the Linear initiative via `/initiative-shape` (per `linear-workflow.md` — goal-directed\n   work is not hand-created in Linear) using the six-field shape below.\n2. Create the issues below, each carrying its repo label, scope, and the technical spec from the\n   Design section, the recommended executor model, **and a self-contained verification-subagent\n   prompt** (see the Verification section — verification is delegated to a subagent, not self-certified).\n\nNo source files are edited in this session. Execution of each issue happens later (own session /\nexecutor), at which point that issue's verification block must pass before it moves to Done.\n\n---\n\n## Linear initiative (six-field shape)\n\n```\nGoal:           For my agents across this workspace, we want governance defined in one place so\n                repos and tools stop diverging — minimal always-on footprint, deep rules on demand.\n\nKey results:\n  KR1 [committed] — Single governance import in the global config.\n     baseline: ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md has 4 rule @-imports + an inline Comments section; no GOVERNANCE.md.\n     target:   exactly 1 governance @-import (rules/GOVERNANCE.md), 0 of the 4 old imports;\n               Comments standard relocated into GOVERNANCE.md; GOVERNANCE.md exists.\n     window:   at initiative close.\n     source:   grep -n \"@\" ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md ; test -f .../rules/GOVERNANCE.md\n\n  KR2 [committed] — Zero restatements / drift across in-scope repos.\n     baseline: 3 AGENTS.md restate the Linear workflow; 1 \"four-field\" drift; 2 repos w/ stale tool names.\n     target:   grep -rn \"## Linear workflow\" across in-scope AGENTS.md = 0;\n               grep -rn \"four-field\" and \"mcp__linear-server__\" across in-scope repos = 0.\n     window:   at close.\n     source:   the grep commands above.\n\n  KR3 [committed] — Every in-scope repo points to the single source exactly once.\n     baseline: 0 repos point to GOVERNANCE.md; nestl/CLAUDE.md does not @AGENTS.md.\n     target:   each in-scope AGENTS.md has exactly 1 rules/GOVERNANCE.md pointer and no restatement;\n               nestl/CLAUDE.md @AGENTS.md.\n     window:   at close.\n     source:   grep -rln \"rules/GOVERNANCE.md\" ; grep -n \"@AGENTS.md\" nestl/CLAUDE.md\n\nAffected repos: agent-skills-shaper, agent-skills, nestl, stock-review\n                + global ~/.claude config (not a repo). EXCLUDED: adyen-onboarding (Tolaria vault).\nAppetite:       ~6 issues  (Small — fits one cycle slot)\nKill condition: hybrid loading proves unreliable in practice — skills/ad-hoc work stop loading the\n                now-on-demand deep rules and principle-grounding visibly degrades. Fallback: revert\n                ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md to eager @-imports of the four rule files.\nProject type:   1 (methodology)\n```\n\n---\n\n## Design (the spec each issue carries)\n\n**Single source of truth:** `agent-skills-shaper/rules/`. Add one thin entry point —\n`rules/GOVERNANCE.md` (~50 lines) — that everything references.\n\nGOVERNANCE.md is **hybrid**: it **inlines** the always-on short rules (Linear lifecycle mechanics +\ncomment standard) and **points** (prose, not `@`-import) to the bulky reference rules with a\n\"read when\" trigger. The Shaper skills already read those on demand.\n\nAnti-drift mechanism, applied everywhere: **define once in `rules/`, reference everywhere, restate\nnowhere.** Each consumer carries a pointer (which can't drift), never a copy.\n\n```\n~/.claude/CLAUDE.md ──@──▶ rules/GOVERNANCE.md ──prose pointers──▶ PRODUCT_RULES.md\nrepo/AGENTS.md ──────────▶ (same file, abs path)                   eng-principles-*.md\n                                                                   linear-workflow.md\n                              inlines: Linear mechanics + comment standard (always-on)\n```\n\n**Uniform governance block** (paste into each in-scope AGENTS.md, swap `<repo>`). **Portable via\nplugin** — no absolute path, because this file gets checked out:\n\n```markdown\n## Workspace governance\n\nThis repo follows the shared governance defined in the **Shaper pack** (`agent-skills-shaper`,\na Claude Code plugin): the Linear initiative/cycle model, product rules, engineering principles,\nand the comment standard. Install the Shaper pack — or read its `rules/GOVERNANCE.md` —\n  to load these rules.\n\nRead it before any Linear work or non-trivial implementation. Do not restate or summarise those\nrules in this file — keep only <repo>-specific instructions (stack, conventions, commands) below.\n```\n\n**Portability model:** absolute paths are confined to the **global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`**, which is\npersonal and machine-specific and is *never* part of a repo checkout — so its `@`-import of\n`/Users/anton/src/agent-skills-shaper/rules/GOVERNANCE.md` (the always-present dev checkout) is safe.\nEvery **checked-out** file (each repo's AGENTS.md) uses the path-free plugin reference above, so a\ncollaborator who clones a single repo gets a working pointer (\"install the Shaper pack\"), not a dead\n`/Users/anton/...` path.
